=================== THE INDUCEMENT, STATED IN FULL ===================
    The object's writes are idempotent, so a first pass that SUCCEEDS settles the message and there is
    nothing left to redeliver. To get a real redelivery of the SAME message this leg made the object's
    LAST DB step fail transiently, from OUTSIDE the service — no code changed, no service config changed,
    no queue property changed, no mock anywhere:

      $ kubectl -n onyxd exec -i postgres-1 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d postgres -X -c \
          "begin; lock table onyx_security.scanner_installations in share mode; select pg_sleep(1800); commit;"

    SHARE mode blocks the lane's INSERT into scanner_installations (writers.py:712-727, reached at
    writers.py:1105) and leaves every SELECT free, so the dumps below are not themselves blocked. While
    a writer waits, the backend is visible:

      $ psql -c "select pid,state,wait_event_type,wait_event,left(query,60) from pg_stat_activity
                  where query ilike '%scanner_installations%' and pid<>pg_backend_pid();"
      58133|active|Timeout|PgSleep|begin; lock table onyx_security.scanner_installations in share mode; s
      58140|active|Lock  |relation|INSERT INTO onyx_security.scanner_installations (device_id, non_employ

    The waiting statement is then cancelled (pg_cancel_backend → SQLSTATE 57014 query_canceled), which
    the lane classifies as TRANSIENT (common/database/pg_retry.py:40 "57014": "statement_timeout"), so
    with_db_retry replays the object (db_retry.py:81-104) and, on exhausting its 3 attempts, propagates —
    the consumer pool then abandons the message and Service Bus re-presents it. That is the broker's real
    at-least-once contract; the only thing induced is an ordinary transient PG write failure.

    WHY THIS INDUCEMENT AND NOT THE OLD ONE. The previous revision induced the abandon by scaling the
    Temporal FRONTEND to zero. On this head that would have made the posture bridge start fail on EVERY
    delivery, so no downstream Temporal start could ever have been REJECTED as a duplicate — and at2's
    own expectation ("downstream Temporal starts rejected as duplicates") would have been unprovable.
    Blocking the object at its last DB step instead leaves Temporal fully up, so delivery 1 really does
    start the downstreams and deliveries 2-4 really do get them rejected (at2-04).

    HONEST SCOPE OF THE INDUCEMENT, so the diff is not overread: the blocked step is
    _write_scanner_installations. BOTH dumps (this one and at2-02) are taken with that ONE step blocked,
    at the identical point of the identical object — so the comparison is like-for-like. What that step
    later wrote, once the lock was released, is disclosed in full at the end of at2-02.

=================== ONE PRE-FLIGHT PROBE, DISCLOSED ===================
    Before this window opened, at 21:06:41Z-21:06:55Z, the queue's `status` was flipped to
    ReceiveDisabled and back to Active to test it as a possible pause knob. It was NOT used in the end
    (the DB-lock inducement above replaced it). The probe ran with active=0 — no message existed yet, the
    scan had not started, and the at2 window opened at 21:13:04Z. The queue's status was Active for the
    whole of both the at2 and at3 windows, and is Active now:
      $ python3 /tmp/prdct11935/r50/qstatus.py
      endpoint-asset-ingestion-prdct11935 status=Active lock_duration=0:05:00 max_delivery_count=5
    Recording it rather than letting a queue-audit trail turn up an undisclosed touch.

=================== WHAT THE DUMP COVERS, stated so its scope is not overread ===================
    Counts plus the CONTENT / STATE / LIFECYCLE columns of every table the lane writes — assets
    (id, name, type, sub_type, unique_identifier, is_deleted, status, version), asset_connections (incl.
    is_deleted), mcp_servers, mcp_ingestion_state (incl. content_hash), agent_skills (skill_md_hash),
    agent_subagents (subagent_md_hash), tools (incl. is_active), usage_sessions, scanner_installations.
    It deliberately does NOT dump pure freshness timestamps such as
    desktop_agent_applications.last_scanned_at: at2 is about resurrection, duplication and content
    rewriting, and that column's behaviour under re-ingest is a separate, documented subject (at7-48).
    Saying so explicitly rather than letting the omission pass unremarked.

    ORDERING FIX MADE THIS ROUND: every SELECT now orders by ALL of its output columns
    (e.g. `order by 1,2,3,4`), not by a key with ties. The previous revision ordered
    mcp_ingestion_state by unique_identifier alone — and this tenant has ~1000 rows sharing one
    unique_identifier — so two dumps of an UNCHANGED table could differ purely by row order. That
    would have made a clean replay look dirty (or, worse, hidden a real change under the noise).
